# Tasks

This is the team's shared task board. The PM creates entries; every agent updates the
status flag for the tasks it touches. This file is the single source of truth for work
state — the `/shell-team:run` loop advances the flag at each phase gate.

## Status flags

`READY_FOR_ARCH` → `READY_FOR_ENG` → `READY_FOR_QA` → `READY_FOR_REVIEW` → `READY_FOR_MERGE`
(or `BLOCKED` / `REWORK` at any stage)

The deliverable is the adjudication itself, and the primary control is the human ratification gate, not a machine gate. One axis is nonetheless held at the higher standard: the four generated blocks and the four `agents/*.md` marker regions must be proven to be genuine `bin/gen-playbook-blocks.sh` output by the same scratch-regeneration diff CI runs, because a hand-edited block is exactly the failure a byte comparison exists to catch.
  - two-stage flow: pass 1 (this) delivers the spec skeleton — predicate, machine pre-filters, recording venue, acceptance-criteria shape — plus the full adjudication companion `.shell-team/specs/T-1020-supersede-adjudication.md`.   - D1–D7 settled in the spec: (1) the retirement predicate, split into (i-a) occasion subsumption, (i-b) protection subsumption, (ii) no `Applies-to` role-coverage shrink, (iii) same `Why` incident class; (2) the six machine pre-filters read out of `bin/check-playbook.sh` (chain target, `loop`→`maintainer` direction, exact-key equality, active target, maintainer entries never shipping, `all` entries hitting four blocks at once); (3) the anti-count-gaming pre-commitment, set before the proposal was written; (4) recording venue is the board plus the companion, with `docs/loop-engineering/lessons-import-disposition.md` deliberately untouched because that ledger is import-scoped by its own opening sentence; (5) exactly two line operations per retiree and no prose edit, which also forbids rescuing a (ii)-blocked pair by widening the successor's `Applies-to`; (6) the regeneration must be proven genuine script output; (7) an entry key inside a `check:` line is single-quoted, because real keys contain backticks and a double-quoted key would be a command substitution inside our own verification command.
  - adjudication result, ahead of ratification: 24 candidates adjudicated across the whole corpus, each with a per-condition verdict and quoted evidence from both entries. **Exactly one satisfies the predicate and every machine constraint — and it is `Scope: maintainer`, so it removes zero bullets from any block.** The other 23 fail on (i-a)/(i-b) (the successor is narrower, or secures a different protection), on (ii) (a named role would lose a rule with nothing replacing it in its block), or on a checker constraint. Cause, measured rather than assumed: nearly every entry carries an explicit positioning sentence against its neighbours, so the deduplication was already done at promotion time.
  - decision points put to the human, each priced with its actual content (not a label): (1) the engineer block's −1 is reachable by exactly one retirement, and only two candidates can do it alone — retiring the whole-artifact-exits-0 rule (losing the nondeterministic-dependency check on regression assertions) or retiring the ratification-language rule (losing the approver-language and presenter-attestation requirements at every human gate); the first is the cheaper in content terms and both are recommended against. (2) the pm-spec block's −11 would require overriding eleven of the seventeen keep verdicts that shrink that block, at least eight of them from the thirteen that do not additionally violate (ii). Recommended outcome: ratify the zero-impact hygiene retirement or decline it, and accept the remaining overage under D3.

  - known boundary, declared rather than discovered: if BOTH copies receive the same patch, parity holds and the parity half cannot see it. - [x] **T-1014** The board-flag rail derives its stops from real telemetry — `READY_FOR_MERGE` — spec: .shell-team/specs/T-1014-flag-rail-data-path.md
  - closed: 2026-08-01, PR #85 → develop, closes #83 — rail lighting visually signed off by the maintainer at the merge gate (AC21 convention); #84 filed as the docs fast-follow
  - source: GitHub issue #83, in full. An issue **was** filed rather than going straight to a spec: T-1012's Non-goals did not predict this item (it is a defect found at that task's merge gate, not a declared fast-follow), it is a distinct bug in a shipped surface rather than a remaining part of issue #77, and #77 must stay open for Part 3 (T-1013) — so folding this into #77 would have made "is #77 done" unanswerable. Base `develop` at `76e64a4`, branch `feature/83-flag-rail-data-path`; the PR references `Closes #83`.
  - why it exists: the rail reads `flagEvents = events.filter(e => !!e.flag)` and **nothing sets `flag`** — `adaptRow()` never derives it, and T-1011's emission instructions never put a `READY_FOR_*` token into any event field. So `flagEvents` is empty for every run that has ever been recorded: the rail can never light and always shows its empty-state caption, and the `bNowFlag` chip is always hidden. Both halves of the data path are missing, which is why this task touches a producer and a consumer in one commit.
  - how it was found, which is itself canon: every static AC T-1012 wrote was green, both QA rounds were environmentally unverified on pixels (no browser reachable in the sandbox), and the cross-provider review raised three Blockers, none of them this. The **maintainer**, performing T-1012's AC29 visual check at the merge gate, saw the empty caption on the full-fidelity fixture. Recorded in `.shell-team/interventions/T-1012.md` entry 2. The design consequence: every static anchor this task adds is one that is **red at the base ref**, and the behavioural proof runs in the existing node harness rather than only in a browser.
  - task classification, stated so review rigour is calibrated rather than guessed: **a two-surface data-path fix plus an extension of a verification mechanism.** The derivation and the emission wording are near the pure-addition class; the harness upgrade (a recording `classList` stub and eight new assertions observing rail state) is verification-mechanism work and carries the higher standard — which is why the pre-commitment below is scoped to that mechanism specifically rather than to the whole task.
  - resolved for the engineer (D1 — which event kinds derive a flag): **all `kind:"event"` rows, uniformly**, inside `adaptRow()`'s event branch; the flag is a property of the `label`, not of the event id. This is issue #83's canon read literally ("an event whose label is one of the five closed flag tokens"). **Rejected — deriving only on `handoff` rows**: it adds a branch the canon does not have and is wrong in a reachable case — `human` is the one id whose `--label` `bin/log-run.sh` *requires*, its vocabulary is free-form (T-1011 D3), and a human settling the board flag at the gate is exactly the row an operator writes; a handoff-only rule silently drops it. Uniformity also keeps the match rule in one place, which is what makes the symmetry table checkable at all. The other four ids' frozen label vocabularies (verdicts, the causing verdict, none) are disjoint from the five tokens, so uniformity costs nothing in practice.
  - resolved for the engineer (D2 — the match rule): **byte-exact whole-label equality** against a `FLAG_STOPS` member — no trim, no case fold, no substring or prefix search, no regex, no Unicode normalization. Frozen identifier `flagFromLabel(label)`; a `null`, absent or non-string label returns null-ish and is never an error. `FLAG_STOPS` stays the **single source** (exactly one occurrence of `READY_FOR_ARCH` in the template — **rejected**: a second literal list inside the derivation, which is how a sixth token gets added to one of them). The emission wording **must** say the label is the bare token with no surrounding prose: without that one sentence the convention and the implementation go asymmetric and nothing in the repo says which side is wrong. Two frozen phrases must appear in `skills/run/SKILL.md` — the bare-token sentence and the byte-exact-equality statement — so the consumer contract is stated at the producer site. A **three-way symmetry table** (emission wording ↔ template match rule ↔ fixture rows) is frozen in the spec across six properties and machine-anchored by one AC.
  - resolved for the engineer (D3 — the `flagStopsReached` rewind): **in scope, not deferred.** Today it is a module-level object `updateRailB` writes into and never clears — invisible while `flagEvents` is always empty, a real defect the moment the rail lights (seeking backward leaves stale reached-dots, and the same `simMs` renders differently depending on how the operator got there, which breaks replay determinism). Frozen: the accumulator is **removed** (zero occurrences of `flagStopsReached`) and replaced by `railReachedAt(simMs)`, called on every `updateRailB` invocation. The ratified rework step-back is **preserved by** the derivation, not lost to it: a later stop reached before a rework stays `reached` while `current` moves back, because both facts follow from "events at or before `simMs`". **Rejected** — leaving the accumulator and filing a follow-up: the defect is unobservable today precisely because the rail never lights, so that route ships a known-broken behaviour into visibility and asks a second round to notice it; the two changes are one line apart in the same function. No follow-up issue is to be filed.
  - resolved for the engineer (D4 — fixtures): a **new** `tests/gen-loop-replay/fixtures/flag-rail/shell-team.jsonl` (run id `20260801T000000Z-flagrail`, 23 rows fully enumerated in the spec's fixture-contract table, every row written by `bin/log-run.sh`) joins the suite's `run_harness` list. **`mixed` stays byte-identical** and keeps proving the other direction — it carries no `READY_FOR` string at the base ref, so it is the standing proof that a flag-less run keeps the ratified empty state. The two fixtures together prove both directions; neither alone does. **Rejected** — extending `mixed` with flag labels: it would delete the empty-state proof in the same commit that makes the empty state newly reachable-but-untested, and would change a fixture whose row counts T-1012's frozen criteria assert. The new fixture carries three near-misses (lowercase, trailing-space, prose-embedded) — the prose-embedded one is the only non-vacuous proof of no-substring-matching — plus a `null`-label handoff (fail-soft) and a `human` row whose bare token is D1's uniformity probe.
  - resolved for the engineer (D5 — change surface): three files plus one fixture. Emission = `skills/run/SKILL.md`'s Telemetry section, the `handoff` bullet, extended by an **indented sub-bullet with zero deleted lines** (the file is a `contain`-mode consumer of four prompt blocks, so a deletion is what breaks prompt-sync, and the sentence keeping `--label` optional lives on the line being extended — additivity is what keeps the fail-soft guarantee from being edited away). Template = `adaptRow()`'s derivation, `FLAG_STOPS`' declaration position, `updateRailB`'s reached derivation. Harness = `tests/gen-loop-replay/run.sh`. **Nothing under `bin/`** — measured: the generator ships rows verbatim (T-1012 D3), so the adaptation is entirely the template's job, and the row schema gains no field (`flag` exists only as an in-page adapted property). `skills/` is deliberately **not** registered in `tests/machine-tokens/run.sh` (its mutation self-check copies only `bin agents templates`, so a `skills/` consumer would make it exit 2 rather than fail red), and no new suite or script file exists, so `.github/workflows/check-handoff.yml` needs **no** edit — the opposite of T-1012's two-coupled-lists hazard, and locked byte-identical.
  - resolved for the engineer (D6 — how the fix is proved): the behavioural proof runs in T-1012's existing node harness, whose extracted slice already contains `buildRailB`/`updateRailB`. Its `makeFakeElement()` `classList` stub becomes **recording** (`add`/`remove`/`toggle` mutate a per-element set, `contains(name)` reads it, and `toggle(name, force)` honours the DOM's optional second argument — without which the rewind assertion cannot work at all, since `updateRailB` clears a dot via `toggle("reached", false)`). The harness exports `flagFromLabel`/`flagEvents`/`FLAG_STOPS`/`updateRailB`/`railStopsB`. **Every static anchor this task adds is red at the base ref** (`flagFromLabel`, `railReachedAt`, the zero-count of `flagStopsReached`, both frozen emission phrases, the new fixture's path); the tokens that are already green (`FLAG_STOPS`, `flagEvents`, `bNowFlag`, the rail CSS) are asserted only as **preservation** locks and labelled as such — the direct lesson of the intervention that produced this task. Eight frozen assertion ids, each with a paired positive control, so a stub that silently failed to record cannot make the rewind assertion pass for the wrong reason.
  - 21 acceptance criteria, **19 mechanical + AC20/AC21 carrying no `check:` by design** (`SKIP` is their expected `check-acs.sh` result — "the whole CI list was actually run plus the mutation self-check was performed", and the rendered-page visual confirmation). Layer split: template derivation and rewind AC1–AC4, emission side AC5–AC8, fixture AC9–AC10, harness surface and behaviour AC11–AC13, the two-direction and symmetry proofs AC14–AC15, negative locks AC16–AC17, records and scope AC18–AC19. No separate issue was filed: #77 already carries all three parts and stays the tracker, the same disposition T-1011 recorded for Part 1, and Part 2's scope is quoted verbatim into the spec's Problem section so the split is auditable against the issue rather than against this board line. Part 3 (README / `docs/` wiring) is T-1013 and is explicitly out of scope here. Base `develop` at `d08393b`, branch `feature/77-part2-gen-loop-replay`; the PR references #77 without closing it (`Closes #77` waits for Part 3).
  - why it exists: T-1011 gave a run a total order — spans and events under one shared monotonic `seq` — and nothing reads it back that way. `rollup-runs.sh` answers "how much" and `cluster-failures.sh` answers "what fails across runs"; neither answers "what happened, in order, in this one run". A ratified prototype page already exists and is hardcoded to one sample run; what is missing is the generator that turns a real run id into that page.
  - task classification, stated so review rigour is calibrated rather than guessed: this is **a new consumer of a frozen contract, plus a new fail-closed generator** — closer to the pure-addition class than T-1011 was, with one exception that is not: the injection/escaping path (D2) and the CI syntax gate (D6) are verification-mechanism work and carry the higher standard, which is why the pre-commitment in the spec is scoped to D6 specifically rather than to the whole task.
  - design-note gating: the Routing Map includes a **ui-designer** step. `design-note-T-1012.md` (in the specs dir) is an input the engineer receives before implementation and is the authority on the *visual* treatment of the degraded states, the empty flag rail, unrecognised-agent styling and non-success-span marking. The spec is the authority on the data contract; a disagreement inside the intent block is a `BLOCKED` report, not a judgment call.
  - resolved for the engineer (D1 — input resolution): the runs **directory** is resolved with precedence `--runs-dir` > `$TEAM_RUNS_DIR` > `$RUNS_DIR` > `team-paths.sh --get runs`, mirroring `bin/log-run.sh:294-297`, with one deliberate divergence taken from `bin/close-out.sh:23-27` — **no guessed literal fallback**, a resolver failure is exit 2, because reading a guessed path would replay the wrong repository. **Every** `*.jsonl` in the directory is scanned in sorted filename order and rows are selected by exact `run_id`; **rejected**: reading only `<runs>/shell-team.jsonl`, which hardcodes this loop's `loop_id` and is already measurably wrong (this repo's runs dir holds `close-out.jsonl` too). Non-`.jsonl` neighbours are ignored, not parsed — measured reachability, not hypothetical: `skills/loop-triage/SKILL.md:43`, `skills/goal/SKILL.md:16`, `bin/consolidate-proposals.sh:134`, `bin/team-init.sh:227` all put non-jsonl files there. Ordering is `seq` ascending with a stable (filename, line) tie-break, because T-1011 declines cross-line validation and a duplicate `seq` must still replay deterministically. T-1011's schema is **consumed, never re-opened**.
  - resolved for the engineer (D2 — injection escaping, the measured top risk): `bin/log-run.sh:234`'s `jesc()` escapes only `\` and `"`, so `<`, `>` and `&` pass through and a `label` containing `</script>` is a **reachable** input (`label` is free-form by T-1011 D3) — raw injection would terminate the script block and kill the page, the same class as the 2026-08-01 `*/` incident. Frozen: exactly **one** injection site, the line `<script type="application/json" id="loop-replay-data">__LOOP_REPLAY_DATA__</script>`, and a generated file with that line normalized back to the placeholder is **byte-identical** to the template — every other run-specific string is written by the page's own JavaScript. The payload is `application/json` consumed by `JSON.parse` in a try/catch (**rejected**: keeping `var DATA = {…}` — injected telemetry would be *code*, and an unparseable payload could not be caught and shown as an error state). Escaping is a blanket substitution of `<`, `>` and `&` to their JSON unicode escapes over the whole payload, provably safe without a parser because none of the three can occur in JSON outside a string. Payload is one compact line; envelope is frozen as exactly `run_id`, `generated_at`, `source` (basenames only — an absolute path is a PII shape), `rows` (**verbatim** rows).
  - resolved for the engineer (D3 — degraded fidelity, open point 1): bash injects, the template's JavaScript adapts (**rejected**: transforming rows in bash, which needs real JSON parsing the zero-dep contract forbids and puts the schema in two languages). The row→affordance mapping is frozen in a table; a non-success span and an unrecognised `span` value are **never dropped**; the roster is the six known ids ∪ the data's distinct `span` values, with `orchestrator`/`human` as structural nodes. Operator-authored `label` text is displayed verbatim in both languages and the prototype's per-`seq` English override table is removed (grounding: T-1010's operator-language boundary). Three degraded shapes are contracted — span-only, event-only, partial — and each must produce a real page that **states its own degradation** through the frozen dictionary key `degradedNoEvents` present in both `ja` and `en`. Span-only is the dominant case, measured: 11 of the 12 runs in this repo's own telemetry at the base ref contain zero event rows. Zero rows cannot occur — that is an unknown run id (exit 1).
  - resolved for the engineer (D4 — output location, open point 2): default `<resolved runs dir>/replay-<run-id>.html`. Measured basis: `templates/shell-team.gitignore:4` and this repo's `.shell-team/.gitignore:4` already ignore `runs/` entirely, so the artifact is git-ignored with **zero new ignore patterns**, and the `.html` suffix dodges both suffix-filtered consumers (`agents/triage-orchestrator.md:33,52,76`'s `<runs>/*.jsonl` glob and `bin/retro-inputs.sh:455`'s `.jsonl` count). **Rejected**: a sibling `<base>/replays/` — it requires editing **both** ignore files (locked by `tests/gitignore-raw-dumps/run.sh`) and leaves every already-scaffolded adopter with an un-ignored directory until they re-run `team-init.sh`. `--out` overrides; overwriting a regular file is fine, a directory/FIFO/**symlink including a dangling one** is refused (`[ -e ]` alone reports a dangling symlink as absent); the run id is validated against `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$` before any path use. **No PII content gate** is added (the playbook's guard is for git-tracked artifacts; this one is ignored by measurement and refusing to render an operator their own telemetry would be coercive). Generation is **manual-only**: `bin/close-out.sh` is untouched, and whether the loop should advertise the command is a Part 3 / T-1013 documentation question, deferred rather than dropped.
  - resolved for the engineer (D5 — fail-closed): exit `0` success, `1` data (no matching row, or a scanned file fails `check-run.sh`), `2` usage/resolution/I-O (bad argument, unresolvable or unreadable runs dir, unreadable template, placeholder count ≠ 1, occupied non-regular output path) — mirroring `check-run.sh`'s own 0/1/2 rather than inventing a third convention. On **every** non-zero path no output file exists: the page is built into a temp file in the destination directory and moved into place last, so "an error, never an empty page" holds by there being no page. Validation runs over every scanned file **before** selection; **rejected**: filter-then-validate, because a structurally broken line may have no extractable `run_id`, belongs to no run, and would be skipped unexamined — fail-open at exactly the spot the issue names.
  - resolved for the engineer (D6 — inline-JS CI syntax coverage, open point 3): the playbook's two questions were answered first. CI **can** verify without duplicating production logic (the artifact under test is the real generator's real output for a committed fixture; the suite extracts the script block and runs `node --check`), and the blast radius of running the real script is bounded and measured (reads two files, writes one under `$TMPDIR`, no git, no network, no cross-PR effect). The zero-dep contract binds **shipped scripts**, not CI jobs — the workflow already curl-pins shellcheck 0.11.0 — and `ubuntu-latest` bundles node, so no adopter gains a dependency. Fail-closed rule: interpreter resolved as `${NODE_BIN:-node}`; missing under `$CI` ⇒ the suite **fails**, missing outside CI ⇒ a printed `SKIP:` line; never a silent pass. **Rejected**: banning `/* */` block comments in the template's JS plus a `*/` grep lock — the prototype carries 33 legitimate block comments documenting the ratified design, converting them churns ~33 documentation-only hunks through review, and the incident class is not reachable here (injected data never lands inside a comment). **Adopted alongside**: a pure-bash structural lock needing no interpreter — injection must not change the file's script-tag arity, and the payload must contain no raw `<` or `>`.
  - resolved for the engineer (D7 — test placement & CI coupling): `tests/gen-loop-replay/` is new, so the **two-coupled-lists hazard is live** in this task (unlike T-1011, which avoided it): `.github/workflows/check-handoff.yml` needs the script and the suite appended to the single-line shellcheck argument list **and** a new `run:` step, both locked by an AC. Fixtures are one directory per scenario (`mixed`, `span-only`, `event-only`, `adopter`, `hostile`, `malformed`, `multi-run`) because the generator scans a whole directory, so a malformed fixture would poison a valid neighbour. `templates/loop-replay.html` is registered as a machine-token consumer in `tests/machine-tokens/run.sh` (the rendered flag rail and verdicts carry `READY_FOR_*`, `PASS`, `FAIL`, `APPROVE`, `REQUEST_CHANGES` verbatim) and proved to bite by mutation; that suite's `--root` idiom copies `bin agents templates`, so a `templates/` consumer is compatible unchanged.

- [x] **T-1011** Run telemetry gains event rows alongside spans — `READY_FOR_MERGE` — spec: .shell-team/specs/T-1011-telemetry-event-rows.md
  - closed: 2026-08-01, PR #81 → develop — issue #77 stays open (Parts 2/3 = T-1012)
  - source: GitHub issue #77, **Part 1 only** — "extend run telemetry with event rows". No separate issue was filed: #77 already carries all three parts and stays the tracker, and Part 1's scope is quoted verbatim into the spec's Problem section so the split is auditable against the issue rather than against this board line. Parts 2 and 3 (`bin/gen-loop-replay.sh`, `templates/loop-replay.html`, README/docs wiring) are T-1012. Base `develop` at `08ebaea`, branch `feature/77-part1-telemetry-events`, PR body carries `Closes #77` only when Part 3 lands — this PR references #77 without closing it.
  - why it exists: telemetry records one thing, a span per sub-agent call. Every transition *between* the calls — a phase hand-off, a route-back, a gate verdict, a human stop or GO, a release — leaves no row, so a run log reads back as a bag of agent invocations rather than as a sequence of what the loop did. Part 2's replay needs the transitions; no inference over span rows reconstructs "a human stopped the loop here". The row shape has to exist first.
  - task classification, stated so review rigour is calibrated rather than guessed: this **writes and extends a verification mechanism itself** (`bin/check-run.sh` is the validator; `bin/log-run.sh` is the only writer and calls the validator in a post-write self-check). Per this repository's playbook that is the long-running class, not the pure-addition class — spec-review rigour is set at the higher standard and a multi-round rework is the expected shape, not a surprise.
  - resolved for the engineer (D1): event rows go in the **same** `<loop_id>.jsonl`, and **both** raw-row consumers learn to skip them. The consumer inventory is measured, not assumed — `grep -c field_str bin/*.sh` returns exactly two files, `bin/rollup-runs.sh` (11) and `bin/cluster-failures.sh` (5); `bin/rollup-track.sh:121` consumes rollup-runs' *stdout* and `bin/retro-inputs.sh:455` only counts `.jsonl` files, so both are outside the blast radius by measurement. **Rejected, with the measurement that kills it**: a `<loop_id>.events.jsonl` sidecar — `agents/triage-orchestrator.md:33,52,76` documents invoking both consumers as `<runs>/*.jsonl`, so a `.jsonl` sidecar is swept into that glob and corrupts them exactly as if it were in the same file, while additionally costing Part 2 a two-file merge. **Also rejected**: the implicit-skip variant (omit `run_id`/`status`/`verdict` so today's consumers ignore event rows untouched) — it genuinely works today (`rollup-runs.sh:77`, `cluster-failures.sh:114`) and is rejected anyway, because replay must group by `run_id` and a skip that happens by accident of field absence is the silent-corruption class the decision exists to remove. The skip rule is **fail-safe**: `kind` absent or `span` is a span, anything else is skipped — an unknown `kind` is by definition not a span, and reporting it is the checker's job, not the reporters'. Neither consumer gains any new output (a new `events: N` line would change a git-tracked `rollup-track.sh` artifact and `consolidate-proposals.sh`'s input).
  - resolved for the engineer (D2): the discriminator is a `kind` key whose **absence means span**, so span rows stay byte-identical (17 keys, same order, no `kind`) and no existing file is invalidated. Closed enum `span|event`; `"kind":"span"` is accepted and validated as a span; any other value, `null`, or unquoted fails closed. Shape mixing is a violation **both** ways: an event row carrying any of the **13 span-only keys**, or a span row carrying `event`. The reverse check is on `event` alone — it is the discriminator's partner key and can mean nothing else, whereas `from`/`to`/`label` are generic enough for a future span-side extension and fall under the tolerated-unknown-key rule. All new event-side key detection is **object-boundary anchored**, reusing `bin/rollup-runs.sh:60`'s precedent; the existing span-side `REQUIRED_KEYS` detection is left byte-identical because changing it would be a span-side behavior change.
  - resolved for the engineer (D3): the vocabulary is issue #77's proposal adopted verbatim as a closed enum — `handoff|rework|gate|human|release` — and the row is exactly 9 keys in a frozen order, with `loop_id`/`run_id`/`seq`/`ts` shared with spans and `seq` a **single monotonic per-run counter across both shapes** so Part 2's replay has a total order. Per-id requiredness is frozen in a table in the spec (handoff: from+to; rework: from+to+label; gate: from+label; human: label; release: none), each `label`-required row justified by the field carrying the event's whole informational content. `from`/`to` stay free-form — an adopter's phases are not this loop's phases.
  - resolved for the engineer (D4): the checker's enforce/tolerate split is enumerated, and **seven reason strings are frozen** so every negative criterion is greppable (`invalid event '<v>' (handoff|rework|gate|human|release)`, `event must be a quoted enum value`, `missing required event key(s):` — deliberately distinct from the span side's wording — `event '<id>' requires non-null <field>`, `event row carries span-only key(s):`, `span row carries event-only key: event`, `invalid kind '<v>' (span|event)` / `kind must be a quoted enum value`). Tolerated and out of contract to flag: unknown extra keys, nullable fields absent or `null`, arbitrary `label`/`from`/`to` text, trailing whitespace, CRLF, blank lines. Structure: `REQUIRED_KEYS` stays the span array byte-identical, the event side gets its own sets, `lint_line` runs the shape-independent checks first and then branches — and the event branch never runs the span required-key/status/verdict checks, because an event row is not a defective span.
  - resolved for the engineer (D5): `--event <id> [--from X] [--to Y] [--label L]`. All **13** span-only flags are usage errors in event mode (exit 2, nothing written) rather than being left for the post-write lint to catch, mirroring D2's mixing rule 1:1; `--event` with `--span` carries the frozen message `--event and --span are mutually exclusive`; `--from`/`--to`/`--label` are usage errors in span mode; an unknown `--event` value and an empty-string required value are usage errors. Version skew (new writer × old checker → row written, exit 1, never rolled back) is **confirmed and re-affirmed rather than redesigned** — it is what the header already documents for every self-check failure, every caller wraps the call in `|| true`, and telemetry is best-effort by contract; it must be written into the header instead of left to be rediscovered.
  - resolved for the engineer (D6): only `skills/run/SKILL.md`'s Telemetry section changes, with each event id mapped to a point where a span is already emitted (`handoff` at the phase boundaries, `rework` at both route-back points, `gate` at the QA/Codex verdicts plus the fail-closed seam gates, `human` at stop/GO/interrupt using the existing `interventions-classes.md` slugs as the recommended label vocabulary, `release` at close-out), alongside the `|| true` and shared-`seq` rules. **Rejected**: per-step reminders through steps 1–7 — one instruction copied seven times is the drift surface `CLAUDE.md` warns about.
  - resolved for the engineer (D7): the shape discrimination stays a **flat single-line scan; no grammar-aware tool is adopted**. The question was asked before the design was settled, because this task extends a verification mechanism. Grounds: `bin/` is pure-bash zero-dependency by contract, so `jq`/python is unavailable in an adopter's repo; and the invariant needs no grammar state — every check is a per-line key test, and `log-run.sh`'s `jesc` guarantees no bare `"` inside a value, so a boundary-anchored key token cannot occur inside a string. The residual (a hand-forged escape sequence) is declared out of scope in the Input space rather than left implicit.
  - resolved for the engineer (D8): **no new test directory, no new `bin/` script, no CI workflow change** — the cases extend four suites that are already in `.github/workflows/check-handoff.yml`'s shellcheck list and already have their own step, so the two-coupled-lists hazard does not arise and the workflow file is asserted byte-identical. The event vocabulary is added to `tests/machine-tokens/run.sh` (same class as every other token group there) and proved to bite by mutation. **Parallel telemetry surfaces dispositioned up front rather than discovered later**: `skills/goal/SKILL.md` and `skills/review-response/SKILL.md` are untouched with stated reasons and locked as a protected invariant, so "you missed a parallel surface" is answered by the frozen intent instead of costing a round.
  - 36 acceptance criteria, **35 mechanical + AC36 carrying no `check:` by design** (`SKIP` is its expected `check-acs.sh` result — the "every CI step was actually run in CI order, and the mutation self-check was actually performed" property). Layer split: writer AC1–AC8, `--line` and back-compat AC9–AC10, checker fail-closed AC11–AC21, consumers AC22–AC25, negative locks AC26 and AC28, instructions AC27, mechanism/CI AC29–AC33, records and scope AC34–AC35. Three deliberate design choices in the criteria: back-compat is proved by running the **base ref's own checker** against every existing fixture and requiring identical exit codes (with a non-vacuity floor of five non-zero fixtures); consumer invariance is proved by `cmp` of stdout against the same file with the event rows deleted, never by a substring; and CRLF tolerance is proved on a **malformed** input, not a well-formed one, so it cannot pass by never being examined. The suites are split across AC21/AC31/AC32 rather than bundled, because of `check-acs.sh`'s 120s per-check cap.
  - hazards pre-empted in the spec's Notes (H1–H11) so none of them forces a re-freeze: `bin/log-run.sh` is a `contain` consumer of `verdict-labels.md` and `skills/run/SKILL.md` of four blocks (registry lines 35/36/38/41 — SKILL.md has no marker region, so the section is free to rewrite but no line elsewhere may be deleted); `tests/errexit-safe/run.sh` pins **six** exit-2 shapes across all four touched `bin/` scripts and statically audits `|| true` guards; the two consumers each carry their own `field_str` copy **by design** and must be fixed independently; `tests/check-run/run.sh` greps five exact span-side stderr phrases that must stay verbatim (which is why the event side gets distinct wording); the two `multi-run.jsonl` fixtures share a basename across different directories; `## Active` is empty on `develop` so this entry must be a pure insertion; and `README.md:172` + `docs/loop-engineering/goal-loop.md:45`/`.ja.md:47` become **incomplete by design** for one task (Part 3 closes them) and must not be read as a defect or fixed here.

  - closed: 2026-07-31, PR #71 → develop, closes #20 — both gates green in 2 rounds each; 1 orchestrator rework pre-QA + 1 Codex Major rework; no re-freeze; fast-follow: 1 Minor waived in place, no issues filed
  - source: GitHub issue #20 — "Operator language and project language are the same setting today, and they should not be". Base `develop` at `4022158`, branch `feature/t-1010-operator-language`, PR body carries `Closes #20`. Scope is issue #20's **axis B only**: axis C shipped with T-1007/T-1008 (do not re-scope), axis D (`bin/gen-project-status.sh` printing Japanese) and the `intent-block` vocabulary (issue #8) stay out and are filed rather than fixed.
  - why it exists: `bin/check-retro.sh` fuses a structure contract to one language's surface form — it full-line-matches five decorated Japanese H2 headings, and `docs/templates/retro-template.md`, the skeleton the scrum-master agent copies, exists only in Japanese. An adopter who has never written a line of Japanese must still emit Japanese headings or the check fails. Three individually-correct rules (the agent mirrors the conversation language, the validator hardcodes one language, `CLAUDE.md` says this repository's files are English) collide on a file class none of them names: the artifacts the loop generates as it runs.
  - resolved for the engineer (D1): **option (iii) — language-neutral section markers**, `<!-- retro-section: keep|problem|try|traps|lessons -->`, one per section, heading text free. The issue's option (i) is rejected on measurement, not taste: `bin/team-init.sh` scaffolds only `<base>/retros/.gitkeep` and never distributes the template, so (i) needs a template-resolution order invented *and* a checker that parses instructional prose in which the same headings appear as both documentation and skeleton. Option (ii) keeps the fusion and needs the configuration surface D2 rules out.
  - resolved for the engineer (D2): **the operator language is not declared** — no env var, no contract field, no `team-paths.sh` key. Under D1 the checker no longer needs to know the language, so a declaration would be a setting with no reader, and `docs/workflow.md` §Language's "zero-config — no language config file and no environment variable" statement stays true instead of being walked back. The rule that replaces the setting goes into `CLAUDE.md` §Language: per-cycle artifacts are in the operator's language, and the language boundary sits at promotion into the shipped corpus (a lesson is authored in English at promotion, never produced by translating an artifact).
  - resolved for the engineer (D3): `.shell-team/retros/2026-07-28.md` is **migrated by pure insertion** — five marker lines, zero deletions, Japanese prose and headings byte-identical. This is a floor, not a target: nothing licenses routing to `maintainer` to satisfy it, exactly as nothing licenses a drop to hit a count. **#58 takes direction (b)** — build the step, and make the claim true by **equality**: the generator's header must carry the CI step's exact name string, derived from the workflow by the criterion rather than restated (a paraphrase would drift the moment the step is renamed). - [x] **T-1007** The lessons ledger is Scope-typed and the shipping boundary is machine-enforced — `READY_FOR_MERGE` — spec: .shell-team/specs/T-1007-scope-typed-ledger.md
  - closed: 2026-07-31, PR #62 → develop — mechanism half of #23 shipped (issue stays open for T-1008, the corpus half); fast-follow #61 filed at the recording step; no re-freeze this task
  - source: GitHub issue #23 — it carries the decided design (the D plan: one ledger, `Scope` mandatory fail-closed, shipped blocks generated from `loop` entries only, maintainer entries bound via `Bound-in`, CI proving provenance), so no new issue was opened: #23 is the tracker. This is the **mechanism half** of a two-task split of #23; the corpus half is T-1008 against the same tracker. Base `develop` at `72b6e8b`, branch `feature/scope-typed-ledger`.
  - why the split, and why this half is first: `bin/check-playbook.sh:592-605`'s catch-all makes any unrecognised `- **Name**:` bullet a fail-closed violation, so a `Scope:`-bearing corpus **cannot exist** before the checker knows the field. This task therefore ships the machine-enforced boundary with **zero corpus** — every new behaviour is proved on fixtures, and all four `templates/prompt-blocks/playbook-*.md` stay byte-unchanged (AC15).
  - why it exists: #23 measured that ~30% of the corpus' active entries are only meaningful when developing this plugin, and they are injected into every adopter's prompts today. `agents/scrum-master.md:52` already classifies retro candidates `[common]` / `[target-specific]`, but `bin/playbook-promote.sh` has no flag carrying that label, so the classification is discarded at promotion. Nothing in the three ledger scripts has any concept of scope: `KNOWN_FIELD_NAMES` is nine fields and neither `Scope` nor `Bound-in` appears anywhere in the repository.
  - resolved for the engineer (DP-a, DP-c, DP-e): `Bound-in` is **mandatory** on `Scope: maintainer` (value-tested — absent and blank are the same violation) and **forbidden** on `Scope: loop` (presence-tested), mirroring the existing `active` × `Superseded-by` asymmetry at `bin/check-playbook.sh:658` and the T-108 round-1 Major that established the value-vs-presence distinction. The enum is pinned at exactly `loop|maintainer` — #23's routing table has four outcomes and two of them (`operator-global`, `drop`) never enter the repository. `--scope` is required with no default (a default silently classifies, the defect at the root of #23); `--bound-in` is passed through, and the combination rules are **not** re-implemented in the promoter — its existing fail-closed re-validation against `check-playbook.sh` rejects an invalid pairing, so there is one authority for the schema and no second copy to drift.
  - **DP-b modifies the tech-lead's recommendation, on the authority of the canonical issue text.** The recommendation was "a `Superseded-by` target must share the superseded entry's `Scope`". #23's stated reason for one file is that splitting "would make a local lesson that generalises unable to name its universal replacement — an un-retirable class" — i.e. `maintainer → loop` supersession is a design requirement, and a symmetric rule would forbid it. The rule adopted is **directional**: only `loop` superseded by `maintainer` is rejected (the graph stays healthy while a shipped rule vanishes with nothing replacing it in the shipped set); `loop→loop`, `maintainer→maintainer` and `maintainer→loop` stay legal. One line: a shipped rule may only be retired in favour of another shipped rule. If the executing side reads #23 differently, that is the one decision to re-open before implementation.
  - resolved for the engineer (DP-d): `Bound-in` is validated by **shape only** — non-empty after trim, not absolute, not `~`-prefixed, plus the same `check_structural` every field value gets — and the checker never reads the filesystem. Existence-checking was rejected because `check-playbook.sh` has no concept of a repository root: a relative pointer would have to resolve against the corpus' own directory (a `$TMPDIR` copy whenever `playbook-promote.sh` re-validates a candidate), against cwd (a verdict that changes with the caller's directory — the one property a validator must not have), or against a discovered git root (a new dependency and a new failure mode). AC4 pins the boundary honestly: a `Bound-in` naming a file that does not exist validates **green**, and the identical corpus gets the identical verdict from two different working directories.
  - DP-f (carried from the tech-lead's measurement): the enforcement goes **into `bin/check-playbook.sh`**, not into a new checker file. A separate checker would have to be invoked by every path that reads the ledger, and nothing would stop one of them forgetting — the "boundary as a convention" failure the D plan refuted — besides adding a `bin/` file, a CI shellcheck argument and a new set of errexit-safe candidate sites. - [x] **T-1006** The lessons file is resolved through `team-paths.sh`, not hardcoded to the legacy layout — `READY_FOR_MERGE` — spec: .shell-team/specs/T-1006-lessons-resolver-key.md
  - closed: 2026-07-31, PR #59 → develop, closes #24 — the #24 prerequisite for #23 shipped; fast-follows #57 #58 filed at the recording step; one ratified v1→v2 re-freeze mid-loop (QA round 1, errexit-safe pin)
  - source: GitHub issue #24 — it already carries the decided shape (canonical path, no team-init scaffold, `--lessons` stays an override, no adopter file moves) and the two reasons it became a prerequisite for #23, so no new issue was opened: #24 is the tracker. Base `develop` at `d691a6f`, branch `feature/lessons-resolver-key`. Lands before #23.
  - why it exists: `CLAUDE.md` §Working rules requires every operating path to be resolved through `bin/team-paths.sh`, and the lessons file is the one exception — `bin/gen-playbook-blocks.sh:93` and `bin/playbook-promote.sh:226` hardcode the **legacy** default, so on the shipped default layout the documented no-argument command points at a directory that does not exist and exits 2. #23 needs both halves fixed: the default command has to work where the corpus will live, and the corpus path has to be namable in CI as `$(team-paths.sh --get lessons)` rather than as a literal.
  - measured correction to the issue: the current key set is **nine** (`base|todo|loops|runs|retros|reviews|specs|provenance|interventions`), not the seven the issue names, so `lessons` makes ten. `tests/team-paths/run.sh:148-164` locks the total three independent ways — and uses the token `lessons` itself as the negative control for "an unregistered key exits 2". Raising the counts without swapping that control leaves a contradiction; swapping it without raising the counts leaves the lock reporting nine. Both halves are one atomic edit (AC2), with `not-a-key` as the replacement token precisely because it can never become a registered key.
  - a third consumer the issue does not name: `bin/retro-inputs.sh` ships four statements asserting no resolver key exists (`:7-10` header framing, `:128-129` help, `:453-454` design comment, `:460` runtime ledger detail). DP-1 resolves this as option (b) — **behaviour unchanged, statements corrected**: switching it to the resolver would drag in the DS-5/DS-6 determination semantics, the canonical `retro-inputs.md` block and its suite, all of which belong with #23's retro side. The one forbidden outcome is shipping a statement that asserts the key does not exist (AC12); the one forbidden side effect is a behaviour change (AC13).
  - resolved for the engineer (DP-2 … DP-6): export variable `TEAM_LESSONS` (a file, so no `_DIR`, and no new file-type suffix convention for one key). ACs say "resolves correctly and the error names the canonical path", never "the command succeeds" — there is no corpus at `.shell-team/lessons.md` until #23, so the bare invocation still exits 2 and a success criterion would be unsatisfiable without absorbing #23. CI gets **one** resolver-level assertion in the existing `Dogfood team-paths` step; the `check-playbook.sh "$(… --get lessons)"` corpus pin is #23's and would fail on every run until then. Fail-closed is capture-then-check on the resolver's exit status **plus** rejection of an empty resolved path — unchecked, `LESSONS="$ROOT/"` is a directory and `gen-playbook-blocks.sh:116`'s `[ -r ]` is true for a directory, so the run would proceed against nothing; `bin/team-init.sh:144-147` is the in-repo precedent. `--lessons` short-circuits the resolver entirely, so an explicit path still works in a repo whose `$TEAM_RUN_BASE` is invalid.
  - the largest breakage risk, invisible until the wiring lands (DP-6): `tests/gen-playbook-blocks/fixtures/root/` holds `tasks/lessons.md` but **no** `tasks/loops/shell-team.contract.yaml`, so the resolver classifies it as the *default* layout and every bare `--root` invocation in that suite starts looking for `.shell-team/lessons.md`. Resolved by creating the legacy marker at runtime in the suite's `clone_fixture` helper and deriving the default-layout variant at runtime too — every other suite in this repo creates that marker at runtime, so a committed one would be the only exception, and no new fixture file enters the scope-lock allow-list.

- [x] **T-1005** The residual merge gate fires on consequence, not on the word "merge" — `READY_FOR_MERGE` — spec: .shell-team/specs/T-1005-tuning-oversight-merge-consequence.md
  - closed: 2026-07-30, PR #55 → develop, closes #26 — the #26 fix shipped; fast-follows #52 #53 #54 filed at the recording step; ratified v1→v2 re-freeze mid-loop (Codex round 1 Major)
  - source: GitHub issue #26 — it names both the defect and the fix criterion, so no new issue was opened: #26 is the tracker. Base `develop` at `1602889`, branch `feature/tuning-oversight-merge-gate`.
  - why it exists: `docs/tuning-oversight.md`'s "fewer interruptions" example ships a residual gate whose three items are "merging", "force-pushing" and "anything that destroys work git cannot restore". The last two are about irreversibility; merging is not, so the gate fires on a **word** rather than on a **consequence**. Merging a retro, a board close-out or a provenance record changes nothing that runs and is revertible in one command; merging `bin/`, `agents/`, `skills/`, `templates/prompt-blocks/`, `CLAUDE.md` or the workflow changes what executes. The page spends the reader's interruption on the first case and gives them no way to tell it from the second.
  - the reason the page must state, not just the rule: **the human is the guarantor at the point where something takes effect.** A records-only merge has no such point, so a gate there costs an interruption and buys nothing. Second-order: the gate is a discipline the operator imposes on themselves so accountability cannot drift — keeping it exactly at the point of real effect is what keeps responsibility where the operator decided it belongs.
  - the trap the page must name, grounded rather than asserted: in a repository whose product is prompt content, "it's only docs" is not a safe test. `templates/prompt-blocks/playbook-*.md` are `.md` files, generated artefacts, and read like documentation — and they are spliced into `agents/*.md`, which ship. `bin/check-prompt-sync.sh` enforces that splice (registry rows 42–45, `marker` mode), and AC8 asserts the mechanism as well as the prose, so the page cannot rot into a claim about a mechanism that has changed. The criterion is whether the content executes, never the file extension.
  - resolved for the engineer (DP-1 … DP-3): the Fixed section is **reconciled, not left to contradict** the corrected example — `Merging waits for a human by the same design.` becomes a statement about **authority** (the loop never merges on its own) with one paragraph distinguishing that from **interruption**, which is the tunable layer; leaving it untouched was rejected because it is the same word-based framing three sections above the fix, and AC3 requires the both-green-gates substance and `no personal setting relaxes it` to survive in the same criterion so the edit cannot become a softening. The "worth keeping in either direction" bullet is rewritten to consequence framing (it is the page's general-principle section, so it outranks the example in influence) while its irreversibility bullet stays unnarrowed as AC4's positive control. The trap paragraph goes in that same general-principle section, after the reason paragraph — the reader carries away the fenced block, not the example's prose.
  - resolved for the engineer (DP-4 … DP-7): the "more checkpoints" example stays a **genuine opposite**, pinned byte-identical to the base ref section-wide rather than protected by a forbidden-token list — so its unconditional "Get agreement before pushing, opening or merging a pull request, or filing an issue" survives by construction. "Kept in step" is defined mechanically: every fenced block byte-identical between en and ja (they are English in both files today because the reader pastes them into their own configuration), surrounding prose Japanese and semantically equivalent, with the extraction diff and a both-directions fence count as AC9. What stayed open was the plumbing class (`shell-construct-aborts-before-ledger-emitted`), and the reason it stayed open is recorded in the spec's `## What changed at v3` as a coordination-layer misclassification rather than a code defect: a reviewer's Blocker label was accepted as a reason to keep the loop open, when deciding which defects stop a change from shipping is not the reviewer's call. Asked "who is blocked by this today", neither defect answers — nine merge commits here, `--last-n` not passed by the role's default, no directory or broken symlink in `.shell-team/reviews/`. AC9 removes the SIGPIPE path in the capped window (opt-in, but a one-line fix is not worth deferring; the measured trigger is bytes near 64 KB, not merge count). AC10 is one bounded regression lock for the class QA correctly recorded at round 3 as having none: one output invariant — heading plus eight complete ledger lines, exit 0 or the usage code — over a **closed list of nine** states, with the count pinned in both directions so the list cannot grow by accretion, and an explicit statement that it claims nothing about undiscovered plumbing defects.   - pm-spec (T-1001, v2): routed back to spec after the cross-provider review returned `REQUEST_CHANGES` and `rework-digest` reported `same-class-repetition` — `unevaluable-condition-reported-as-benign`, three times. The three defects were not three bugs: v1 made a benign status the default and asked for one hand-written guard per adverse condition, so the class recurred wherever a guard was missing. v2 inverts the default instead of adding a fourth guard — `unavailable` is the initial value of every ledger line, `read` and `empty` are confined to two promotion functions with exactly eight call sites, probing is separated from emission, and each promotion site carries an inventory row stating what is emitted when its determination cannot be made plus a fixture that makes that determination impossible. The checker takes the same inversion: "located and validated", "absent" and "could not be read" become three outcomes that cannot coincide, cross-checked between two independent determinations. Criteria 30 → 33; the skeleton QA already verified (git-derived window, `gh` demoted to enrichment, eight-input ledger, region walk) is unchanged on purpose. Full rationale in the spec's `## What changed at v2`.

The sixth — `sanitize()` not removing Unicode line separators or bidi controls — is declined in Non-goals with three reasons, none of them "the reviewer called it a follow-up": those characters are not record separators for any POSIX text tool so AC13's property holds without them, the residual display-level risk belongs to a repository-wide content guard over every tracked file rather than to one emitter, and `tr -d` cannot delete a multi-byte code point at all so a fix means changing the sanitiser's tool in the same round as the status class. Filed as its own issue.

Four mechanics the spec's author could not execute (pm-spec has no shell) were verified empirically rather than by reasoning: AC6's `awk -F' — '` multibyte field separator does yield NF=3 per ledger line and its read-must-carry-a-digit guard does bite on a digitless detail; AC2's `LC_ALL=C tr -d '[:print:]\t\r\n'` does leave bytes behind for a non-ASCII file and nothing for an ASCII one; AC19's `--print` regex tolerates the column padding, which `provenance` at ten characters overruns; AC3's expected field layout matches `registry.txt`'s actual `mode block consumer…` shape.

Did not touch the decision-layer inversion, the checker's cross-check, the git-derived window, `gh` demotion, the eight-input ledger, round-1's fixes, or AC30/AC33's bare-name convention — all confirmed unchanged. **Fix 1 (AC8)**: `count_dir_entries()`'s `case *"$suffix") [ -f "$f" ] && DIR_N_MATCH=... ;; esac` — a suffix-matching name that fails the regular-file test (a directory or broken symlink ending in `.md`) made the whole case statement return non-zero, and the bare call site in `report_dir_input()` died under errexit. Fixed with an `if` inside the case arm plus an unconditional `return 0` at the function's end — verified BOTH are needed: a mutation reverting only the case-arm shape (leaving the trailing `return 0`) did not reproduce the crash, since the unconditional return masks the loop's exit status; reverting both did, and `tests/retro-inputs/invariants.sh`'s first state caught it (`FAIL: ... exit status 1`). Also fixed the same shape's three occurrences in `probe_cycle_window()` (the fallback note, both qualifier lines) — harmless there only by execution-order accident. Two of my own explanatory comments initially self-hit AC8's own forbidden-pattern regex (a self-hitting-grep hazard) and were reworded to prose before hand-off. **Fix 2 (AC9)**: the capped-window pipeline (`printf ... | head -n "$last_n"`) gave the writer a SIGPIPE past ~64KB of merge log under pipefail; replaced with `awk -v n="$last_n" 'NR <= n' <<< "$log_out"` — a here-string has no live writer process to signal. Reproduced the crash directly first (a throwaway repo, 200 merges with 300-byte padded branch names, ~76KB log; `git log ... | head -n 5` alone under `set -euo pipefail` exits 141), then confirmed the fix resolves it (`--last-n 5` against the same repo now exits 0, cap applied). This also let the old `--last-n 0` special case be deleted: `NR<=0` is never true, so zero is handled with no separate branch. **New lock (AC10)**: `tests/retro-inputs/invariants.sh`, one shared `assert_invariant()` helper (heading + 8 ledger lines + exit 0-or-2) run identically across the 9 closed-list states named in the spec — deliberately does NOT assert per-state status content, to avoid quietly widening the lock's claim into decision-layer territory `tests/retro-inputs/run.sh` already covers. `bash bin/check-acs.sh .shell-team/specs/T-1001-retro-input-acquisition.md` → 30/30; `check-intent` aligned (v3, `1308789fb1b11d6fb6f5f349731ab2df66bdaed8`, unchanged — intent block untouched); `check-provenance` conformant (20 decisions, 5 new this round); `check-prompt-sync` green; shellcheck clean on the CI list including `invariants.sh`; all named suites + the new invariants suite green; `.github/workflows/check-handoff.yml` updated (shellcheck arg + 2 new steps). ## Format

```markdown
- [ ] **T-XXX** <one-line title> — `<STATUS_FLAG>` — spec: <specs-dir>/<slug>.md
  - <optional latest note from whichever agent touched it last>
```

`<specs-dir>` is `.shell-team/specs/` by default (or `docs/specs/` in a legacy
`tasks/` layout). The hand-off linter accepts any spec path ending in `.md`.
